16:I[44692,["/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/08cykig6vtx_p.js","/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/07qi2okp-.yp9.js","/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/0frujd.eyhz4o.js","/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/0n4z.7yu76je-.js","/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/09-o.77-p0p36.js","/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/1304s0wcp2kce.js","/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/0j1uy9t~zxcf0.js","/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/14_5o-nbg8k_r.js"],"DailyView"]
17:T46b,The paper formulates Z-loss as a backward source. For a penalty of the form λ(log Z - c)^2, the logit gradient is proportional to 2λ(log Z - c) times the softmax distribution. This source then gets transported by the architecture. The analysis covers several coordinates. First, cross-entropy is invariant to a common shift of all logits, while Z-loss is not, so raw-logit Z-loss acts along a CE-invisible direction; centering the output head changes this coordinate while preserving CE. Second, tied embeddings create a coupled gradient path because the same matrix is used for input embeddings and output projection. Third, the output-to-hidden gain can amplify or reshape the Z-loss source before it reaches hidden states and parameters, motivating spectral audits and gain-aware weighting. Fourth, top-k MoE routers introduce reduction-scale and routing-scale distortions, so the same router Z-loss coefficient can mean different effective forces under different routing setups. Fifth, fused CE+Z-loss kernels need source-consistency checks, since matching the forward scalar is not sufficient if the backward source differs.18:T5c5,The base setup is masked diffusion language modeling. During training, completion tokens are masked according to a sampled time t, and the model predicts the original tokens at masked positions. During inference, a chunk of length C starts fully masked; the model iteratively predicts masked positions in parallel and keeps selected high-confidence predictions until the chunk is filled. Registers are fixed-position tokens inserted into the prompt. After a chunk is denoised, an additional forward pass over the prompt and completed chunk saves the last-layer hidden states at the register positions, producing an R-by-d continuous state. For the next chunk, those saved states replace the input embeddings at the same register positions, while the rest of the prompt remains unchanged. The same slots are reused after every chunk, so the model must learn what to preserve, what to overwrite, and how to expose that state to the next denoising process. The supervised recipe splits a long reasoning trace into chunks, clears generated text between chunks, and trains the next chunk from the prompt plus carried register state. To prevent shortcut learning, the training sometimes masks attention from the new chunk to the prompt and forces prediction from a fully masked chunk. The work also compares task-directed registers trained by next-chunk loss with reconstruction-trained memory tokens, and integrates registers into chunked GRPO for reinforcement learning refinement.19:T40d,Read closely for about 60 to 90 minutes. Start with Figure 1 to understand the bounded-state setting: generated text is cleared, and the carried state size does not grow with the number of chunks. Then read Figure 2 and the method section for the inference loop: denoise a chunk, run an extra forward pass, save register hidden states, and inject them into the next chunk. The most important technical material is the training recipe, especially text clearing, prompt-attention masking, and fully masked prediction. These choices create the need to use registers. In the experiments, focus on the comparisons against discrete-text carry, reconstruction memory tokens, and full-sequence SFT. The GRPO section is useful for seeing whether RL improves the register read-write policy rather than only improving local chunk generation. Good follow-up questions are register-capacity scaling with R and d, interpretability of the carried state, cross-task transfer, and whether the same objective can be moved into pretraining or mid-training.1a:T8a6,方法叫 EB-Decode，核心由两个可学习模块组成。

第一部分是 Learnable Block Size, LBS。作者的观察是，token entropy 沿位置分布常呈现类似 staircase 的结构，说明相邻甚至非连续位置上，可能存在一批难度接近、语义上也相对一致的 token，它们适合被视作同一批次提前解码。与固定长度 block 或基于分隔符的启发式切分不同，LBS 用一个轻量网络读取每个位置的统计量，主要包括 token entropy、位置信息以及语义相关特征，在线预测 block grouping。论文强调它既支持 contiguous block，也支持 non-contiguous grouping，这一点比传统“滑窗式并行”更灵活。其目标不是做语法 chunking，而是做 difficulty-aware clustering：把收敛速度相近的 token 放在一起，避免 easy token 被 hard token 拖慢。

第二部分是 Learnable Parallel Sampling, LPS。现有 confidence-based decoding 的问题在于：只有当 token 置信度超过阈值时才 finalize，而很多 token 在更早步骤就已经预测正确，只是概率还没高到阈值，因此会被继续 remask 和重复预测。LPS 试图学习一个 position-aware 的 finalize policy。它利用每个位置在当前 step 的 entropy、位置编码、step index 等特征，预测该位置是否已经“足够收敛”，从而允许早于传统阈值进行定稿。因为 LBS 已经把难度相近 token 组织成 block，LPS 可以在 variable-length block 上执行并行 finalize，而不是受固定 block 边界限制。

从机制上看，这篇论文不是改 dLLM 主干模型，也不是重新训练一个新的 diffusion objective，而是在 decoder 侧外挂两个轻量路由/决策网络。这样做的好处是兼容现有 dLLM 和已有 KV-cache pipeline，代价则是需要额外训练这两个小网络，并且其泛化能力会受所用 backbone、任务分布和采样设置影响。与已有方法相比，区别主要有三点：一是 block size 从 heuristic 变成 learned；二是 early finalization 从固定 confidence threshold 变成 learned policy；三是 block 组织和 finalize 决策是联动的，而不是各自独立的后处理规则。1b:T7f5,The method, EB-Decode, has two main components.

The first is Learnable Block Size (LBS). The authors observe that token entropy across positions often follows a staircase-like pattern, suggesting that some positions—contiguous or even non-contiguous—share similar uncertainty and semantic coherence. Instead of using fixed-size blocks or heuristic boundaries such as delimiters, LBS uses a lightweight network to predict block groupings on the fly from token-level statistics, including entropy, positional information, and semantic cues. The goal is not syntactic chunking. It is difficulty-aware grouping: easy tokens should not be delayed by harder ones simply because they happen to sit in the same fixed block.

The second is Learnable Parallel Sampling (LPS). Standard confidence-based decoding finalizes a token only when its confidence exceeds a threshold. The paper argues that this wastes computation because many tokens become correct well before their confidence reaches that threshold. LPS learns a position-aware finalization policy using per-token features such as entropy, position, and denoising step. It predicts which positions have already converged enough to be finalized early. Because LBS already organizes tokens into variable-length groups, LPS can operate under adaptive blocks rather than fixed boundaries.

A key design choice is that the paper does not modify the diffusion LM backbone or training objective. Instead, it adds lightweight decision modules on top of the decoder loop. This makes the method easier to integrate with existing dLLMs and compatible with KV-cache pipelines. The trade-off is that these auxiliary networks need to be trained, and their robustness may depend on the backbone, task distribution, and sampling setup. Relative to prior work, the main differences are: learned rather than heuristic block sizing, learned early finalization rather than fixed confidence thresholds, and joint handling of grouping and finalization rather than treating them as separate post-hoc rules.1c:T62b,The evaluation covers three diffusion LLMs and four representative benchmarks. The headline result is a 3.53× to 18.76× throughput improvement over the vanilla decoder while maintaining comparable accuracy, with only about 6.21% routing overhead. When combined with a KV-cache pipeline, EB-Decode provides an additional 1.20× speedup.

The motivation is grounded in a real deployment gap. The paper cites that LLaMA3-8B-Instruct reaches 48.0 tokens/s on an A100-PCIe 40GB GPU, while LLaDA-8B-Instruct reaches only 3.5 tokens/s. It also notes that reducing denoising steps from 1,024 to 256 can drop accuracy from 83.5% to 54.1%. EB-Decode is meant to avoid this blunt speed-quality trade-off by freezing already-converged tokens instead of simply cutting the number of steps.

The most informative ablations are likely the following. First, LBS-only and LPS-only variants, which show whether the gain comes mainly from better grouping or from more aggressive early finalization. Second, comparisons among fixed blocks, heuristic adaptive blocks, and learned blocks, which test the paper’s central claim about heterogeneous token difficulty. Third, experiments combining EB-Decode with KV caching, which clarify whether the method replaces or complements existing systems optimizations. According to the paper, it complements them. The provided full-text excerpt does not include all detailed tables, so it would be unsafe to invent per-benchmark numbers, but the main empirical message is clear: the gains are primarily in throughput, and quality degradation is kept modest.1d:T575,This is a skim paper rather than a line-by-line read. Start with the introduction and make sure the two core observations are clear: token difficulty is heterogeneous, and many tokens are effectively correct before they cross the confidence threshold. Then read the method section with attention to the inputs and training signals for LBS and LPS, and how they are inserted into the denoising loop. The most important implementation questions are how blocks are represented, whether non-contiguous grouping is actually efficient in practice, and whether LPS is trained from explicit supervision or distilled from downstream correctness.

In the experiments, focus on three things: throughput-versus-accuracy trade-off plots, comparisons among block-selection strategies, and results when combined with KV-cache pipelines. If time is limited, the main table plus the ablation section should be enough; roughly 25–35 minutes should capture the key ideas.

Two points are worth deeper scrutiny. First, the entropy-staircase observation: does it hold consistently across code, math, and general instruction-following, or only in certain distributions? Second, error propagation: once a token is finalized too early, can that mistake contaminate later denoising because diffusion decoding is bidirectionally coupled? If the paper does not analyze this carefully, that becomes a key reproduction risk.1e:T740,The method, ARIA, stands for Autoencoder-gated Inference-time Unlearning. It has three main components. First, the authors pretrain sparse autoencoders on general corpora to decompose hidden activations from the frozen base model into sparse latent features. SAE latents are used instead of raw hidden states because they are more interpretable and sparse, which makes downstream detection easier to audit and less entangled. Second, they train a lightweight sparse linear detector on top of these frozen SAE latents to recognize forget-related generation states. Importantly, the detector operates over generation trajectories rather than only classifying prompts, so it can catch leakage that appears through paraphrases, intermediate reasoning, or indirect continuation patterns. Third, at inference time, the detector gates the next-token policy. If the detector is not triggered, decoding follows the original frozen model exactly. If it is triggered, ARIA applies a suppressive intervention to the token distribution to block exposure of the target knowledge. This is the key difference from weight-editing approaches: the intervention is local, conditional, and state-dependent rather than global and permanent. The theoretical section provides a useful decomposition: forget leakage is bounded by detector false negatives, while retain degradation is bounded by detector false positives. That turns the problem into learning a high-quality state detector rather than searching for a single ideal weight update. The feature-level analysis is also notable: because the detector is sparse and linear over SAE features, individual triggers can be attributed to a small set of layer-specific latents. The paper uses this to show that some residual retain loss may come from stylistic artifacts in unlearning data rather than the target knowledge itself.1f:T57d,The evaluation covers three established unlearning benchmarks, TOFU, R-TOFU, and WMDP, on two model families: DeepSeek-R1-1.5B as a thinking model and Gemma-3-1B-it as an instruction model. Against weight-based baselines, ARIA consistently shows a better forget–retain trade-off. The headline result reported in the introduction is on WMDP-cyber: ARIA pushes forget-set accuracy below all competing methods while keeping MMLU within about a 1% drop from the original model. That matters because it indicates the method is not simply degrading the model broadly to appear safer. The robustness section is especially important. The authors introduce three post-unlearning adversarial attacks spanning both weight-space and decoding-space recovery attempts, including lightweight post-unlearning fine-tuning and decoding-time recovery strategies. ARIA remains more robust under these attacks, which is plausible given that the base weights are left untouched and the control mechanism lives at inference time. The ablations are more informative in trend than in one single number: sparse linear detection over SAE latents is already sufficient to separate many forget-related states; retain loss tracks detector false positives more closely than intervention strength alone; and the interpretability case study suggests that benchmark artifacts can conflate response style with actual knowledge forgetting.20:Tacd,方法核心是把不同 recurrent depth 的 next-token 分布 p_r 看成对最终分布 p_R 的逐步逼近，并在“最终验证尚未完成”时，提前用较浅深度的预测启动后续 token 的计算。具体有三层设计。

第一层是单分支自 speculative。对当前前缀 X，模型在较早深度 r1 产生第一提案 token。系统不等待该 token 走完整个 R 深度，而是立刻把它接到前缀后面，开始计算下一个位置的一部分 recurrent steps。等原始分支到达最终深度 R 后，再按标准 speculative decoding / rejection sampling 规则验证该提案。如果接受，后续 token 已经“预跑”了一段深度，因此能节省延迟；如果拒绝，则回退到已验证前缀。

第二层是论文的关键改进：residual second proposal。作者观察到 Looped Transformer 的更深中间状态通常更接近最终分布，于是在更深深度 r2 再给出第二个提案，并为它启动一条备用 continuation。这样当第一提案被拒绝时，第二提案对应的后续计算不一定作废，可以保留一部分进度。这里不是简单并行两个独立 draft，而是利用 residual distribution 构造第二提案，使其覆盖第一提案未解释的概率质量。文中给出严格的采样与验证规则，保证最终生成分布与目标模型完全一致。

第三层是 gating。双提案虽然能降低重启损失，但也会增加无效计算。作者因此只在深层分布与第一提案不一致时，才创建 fallback speculative continuation。也就是说，第二分支不是总开，而是按中间深度的分歧信号触发。这个设计的目的很直接：在 acceptance rate 已经高的区域，避免为了保险而额外占用算力。

理论部分有两个值得注意的点。其一，作者证明 restart probability 只依赖第二提案深度 r2，而与第一提案深度 r1 无关。直觉上，真正决定“第一条 speculative 路径失败后是否还得完全重启”的，是更深备用提案离最终分布还有多远。其二，作者基于实测的 intermediate-to-target TV distance 随深度呈幂律衰减，推导出最优 proposal depths 的闭式解，并与实验最优点基本吻合。这使得 r1、r2 的选择不再只是经验调参，而有了可解释的深度分配原则。

工程实现上，论文在 SGLang 中实现了 branch-wise KV cache 管理和 CUDA Graph 优化。因为不同分支处在不同 token 位置和不同 recurrent depth，KV 复用与裁剪是系统成败的关键。作者把原始分支和 speculative continuations 组织成可批处理的异步流水线，让共享层在一个 batch 中处理“不同分支、不同深度”的状态，从而提升硬件利用率。21:Tb3e,The method treats the intermediate next-token distributions p_r produced at recurrent depth r as increasingly accurate approximations to the final target distribution p_R. Instead of waiting until depth R is completed for the current token, LoopSpec samples an early draft from a shallower depth and immediately starts partial computation for the next token conditioned on that draft.

The first component is single-branch self-speculation. Given a prefix X, the model produces a first proposal at depth r1. Before the original branch reaches final depth R, the system appends that proposed token and starts computing recurrent steps for the following position. Once the original branch reaches p_R, the proposal is verified using exact speculative decoding / rejection sampling. If accepted, some work for the next token has already been done; if rejected, the computation rolls back to the verified prefix.

The second component is the main algorithmic addition: a residual second proposal from a deeper depth r2. Since deeper recurrent states are typically closer to the final distribution, LoopSpec generates a second proposal and launches an alternative continuation before the first proposal is fully verified. This is not just running two independent drafts. The paper defines the second proposal through a residual distribution construction so that probability mass not covered by the first proposal is handled correctly, while preserving exactness of the final sampling distribution.

The third component is gating. A fallback continuation is only created when the deeper recurrent state disagrees with the first draft. This avoids paying the overhead of a second speculative branch when the first proposal is already likely to be accepted. In other words, the system uses disagreement between intermediate depths as a trigger for extra speculative work.

The theory section is stronger than a typical systems paper. First, the authors prove that the restart probability depends only on the depth of the second proposal, not on the first proposal depth. Intuitively, once the first branch fails, what matters is how close the backup branch is to the final distribution. Second, they empirically observe a power-law decay in the TV distance between intermediate and final distributions, and derive closed-form optimal proposal depths from that decay. The predicted optima match measured best settings well, which gives a principled way to choose r1 and r2.

On the implementation side, the paper integrates LoopSpec into SGLang with branch-wise KV-cache management and CUDA Graph optimization. This matters because different speculative branches sit at different token positions and recurrent depths. The system batches original and speculative continuations together despite their asynchronous progress, which is what turns the algorithm into an actual throughput gain.22:T6a9,The evaluation spans seven checkpoints from two Looped Transformer families and reports end-to-end lossless decoding speedups in a real serving stack. The headline number is up to 6.83× exact speedup, and up to 1.7× faster than the training-based DFlash baseline. That is a meaningful result because it suggests that, for Looped Transformers, exploiting intermediate recurrent states directly can outperform methods that rely on an explicitly trained drafter.

The more informative results are in the ablations. Proposal-depth sweeps show a stable optimum region, and the empirically best depths align with the closed-form predictions derived from the TV-distance decay model. The residual second proposal substantially reduces the penalty of first-proposal rejection, which indicates that it is central to the pipeline’s robustness rather than a minor add-on. The gating mechanism improves the speedup/overhead trade-off by suppressing unnecessary fallback branches when acceptance is already high. System-level optimizations, especially branch-wise KV handling and CUDA Graphs, also contribute materially; without them, the algorithmic gains would not fully translate into wall-clock speed.

A useful empirical takeaway is that LoopSpec’s benefit depends on how quickly intermediate recurrent distributions approach the final one. If shallow depths already have strong token agreement with the final prediction, the pipeline can start useful future work early. If deeper depths converge much faster, the second proposal becomes an effective safety net. So the reported 6.83× should be read as architecture- and depth-schedule-dependent, not as a universal multiplier for all shared-weight models.23:T4af,For a skim-level read, focus on the mechanism rather than every proof detail. Start with the introduction and Figure 1 to understand the execution timeline: original branch, first proposal, second proposal, and how verification interacts with pipelined continuation. Then read Sections 4.2 and 4.3 carefully, because the residual second proposal and the gating rule are the real contributions. If you only read the abstract, it is easy to misread the method as simply adding another speculative branch; the paper’s value lies in how exactness is preserved and when the fallback branch is worth launching.

Section 5 is worth reading even on a first pass. The closed-form depth selection is useful beyond this paper because it links intermediate-to-final distribution convergence to speculative scheduling. If you work on recurrent, shared-weight, or iterative refinement LMs, that analysis is portable.

In the experiments, prioritize the main speedup table, proposal-depth ablations, gating ablations, and the appendix material on KV-cache management. Around 30–45 minutes should be enough to extract the main ideas; spend longer on the appendix if serving implementation details matter to you.24:T5de,Blindspot is built as a live simulation framework rather than a fixed set of pre-generated trajectories. Each scenario specifies an initial world state, available tools, policies, user objectives, and safety-relevant conditions. A User Agent interacts with a Target Agent over multiple turns, and in adaptive attacks the next user action is generated conditioned on the target agent’s actual behavior and the current attack state. The Target Agent acts in a stateful tool environment where tool calls, outputs, and resulting state transitions are explicitly recorded. This matters because the benchmark can judge what the agent actually executed, not just what it claimed in text. The current release includes 22 attack families and 35 scenarios across 7 domains, with 25 core-domain and 10 cross-domain scenarios. The dataset contains more than 2,500 long-horizon trajectories with an average length of 14.7 turns. The adjudication protocol is a major design point. Instead of a binary success/failure label, the benchmark defines five trajectory-level outcomes: Safe Completion, Correct Refusal, Unsafe Completion, Over-Refusal, and Indeterminate. These labels are assigned through an evidence-grounded pipeline that combines policy checks, authorization checks, tool-execution traces, environment-state validation, semantic judges, and human review when needed. This allows the benchmark to distinguish textual refusal from actual execution and to evaluate safety against real environment effects.25:T430,The benchmark is used to evaluate 13 proprietary and open-weight models with 8 trajectory-level metrics covering unsafe completion, appropriate refusal, benign utility, over-refusal, execution incompleteness, repeated-run robustness, and post-refusal failure. The excerpt does not include the full numeric tables, but the qualitative findings are already clear. First, model safety is not captured by a single scalar: some models reduce unsafe completion at the cost of high over-refusal, while others remain helpful but cross safety boundaries once authorization, tool state, or external content changes over time. Second, long-horizon tool environments reveal failure modes that are largely invisible in one-shot benchmarks, especially attacks involving intent hijacking, objective drift, memory poisoning, and indirect prompt injection. Third, repeated-run robustness matters: the same model can behave inconsistently across rollouts, which is a serious deployment concern for agents. The main experimental value is therefore diagnostic rather than leaderboard-centric.26:T666,The main artifact is the ImpossibleRubrics benchmark. Instead of storing fixed rubrics, it stores task environments: each example contains a closed evidence packet, a question designed to pressure the model toward a confident answer, and a machine-checkable oracle certificate specifying which claims are permitted and which are prohibited. This design matters because a fixed-rubric benchmark would be vulnerable to memorization and would not truly test rubric generation quality. By keeping environments and certificates, the benchmark can evaluate newly generated rubrics from arbitrary models. The evaluation protocol is adversarial but structured. A rubric generator first produces a rubric from the task and evidence. A fixed attacker then writes an answer intended to score highly under that rubric, regardless of whether it remains faithful to the evidence. An honest baseline answer is also prepared. A literal judge scores both answers using the generated rubric, while the oracle certificate independently checks for violations. Exploitation is defined when the attacker scores above the honest baseline under the rubric judge while failing the oracle certificate. This turns rubric exploitability into a measurable event rather than an anecdotal concern. The paper also reports multiple robustness analyses to isolate whether failures come from the judge, the oracle, the attacker, or the rubric generator itself. A particularly important calibration is the fixed-rubric setting: certificate-faithful fixed rubrics are exploited 0 out of 45 times, suggesting the issue is rubric quality rather than unavoidable task ambiguity.27:T50f,The benchmark contains 169 impossible tasks and 48 answerable controls across six categories. The paper evaluates 11 rubric generators and finds a clear exploitability gradient: stronger models are exploited on roughly 8%-15% of the stress split, mid-tier models around 17%-18%, and mini models around 26%. Two points stand out. First, the frontier is far from saturated: stronger generators reduce exploitability but do not eliminate it. Second, an open-weight generator matches contemporary closed models on the stress cut, which suggests the bottleneck is not only model scale but also how models formulate reward criteria under evidence constraints. The motivating ivermectin example is especially informative. The evidence packet contains no prophylaxis trials, so an honest answer should state that no numerical preventive probability can be derived. Yet a generated rubric explicitly awards full credit for answering 0.0%, effectively converting absence of evidence into evidence of zero effect. An attacker follows that flawed rubric and receives 100/100, while the honest baseline gets only 36/100. The oracle certificate correctly flags the attacker as invalid. This example captures the central failure mode: the rubric generator writes unsupported inference into the reward function.28:T471,Two-layer design:
1) AraBehave benchmark: 1,623 culturally grounded Arabic prompts focused on open-ended behavior. Six models are answered and rated by native Arabic speakers from five regions on a 1–5 Likert scale, with mandatory written rationales. Total: 29,214 judgments; inter-rater reliability ICC=0.58. Importantly, the paper’s error taxonomy and conclusions are derived from annotator rationales rather than author impressions or LLM judges.
2) Automated scorer: a regression-based judge model trained on the human labels. Generalization is tested via leave-one-model-out (train on labels from 5 systems, predict on the 6th), achieving Pearson r=0.74 on unseen systems. The scorer is then used for controlled, higher-throughput analyses (system prompts, language switching, scaling, instruction-tuning variants).
Analytical framing: decompose failures into (a) normative stance/framing errors (secular/relativist “both-sides” answers for culturally settled issues) vs. (b) grounded cultural inaccuracies (notably scriptural errors). The “same overall score, opposite failure modes” result motivates the decomposition.29:T49e,Key empirical points are diagnostic contrasts rather than leaderboard chasing:
- Six-model evaluation (3 Arabic-centric + 3 frontier general models). Top general and top Arabic-centric models tie in overall score (Gemini 3.1: 3.84; Fanar 2.0: 3.83) but fail differently: general models are frequently penalized for Western framing (~28% of rationales) and rarely for scriptural errors (~6%), while Arabic-centric models show the reverse pattern (~6% vs. ~29%).
- “Stance is cheap”: adding a culturally grounded system prompt boosts general models sharply (e.g., Gemini 3.1: 3.92→4.57; GPT-5: 3.47→4.15). A generic helpfulness prompt can also degrade scores substantially (example shown: 3.47→2.79).
- “Grounding tracks capability/data”: scaling from 2B→27B improves scores (2.72→3.77), consistent with grounding being harder and more dependent on model capacity and coverage.
- Language effect: asking in English instead of Arabic drops performance for 5/6 models (example: 3.86→3.34), suggesting language-conditioned alignment/behavior.
- Safety ≠ cultural appropriateness: safety scores are high (89–99) while cultural scores remain moderate (2.71–3.84).2a:T7d1,The paper first provides a mechanism-level diagnosis, then introduces a method. The diagnosis centers on how the so-called Lucky Hit mechanism behaves under different answer spaces. Standard TTRL implicitly assumes that if the model samples multiple reasoning trajectories, the correct answer is more likely to become the majority and therefore serve as a useful pseudo-label. The authors argue that this assumption is much more plausible in large free-form answer spaces, where incorrect trajectories tend to disperse across many distinct strings. In a small multiple-choice space, however, different wrong rationales can collide on the same wrong option, creating a false consensus that is then reinforced by training. The model becomes more answer-consistent without becoming more correct.

Based on this diagnosis, the paper proposes PROSE: Process Reward Guided Self-Training. PROSE keeps the basic test-time RL loop—sample trajectories on unlabeled test questions, compute rewards, update the policy—but replaces majority-vote answer reward with process-level reward. A medical process reward model scores each reasoning step, and the trajectory reward is defined as the minimum step score rather than the mean. This design choice is central. Minimum aggregation prevents a trajectory from hiding one critical reasoning error behind many fluent or locally correct steps. If the weakest step is poor, the whole trajectory should not receive a high reward. The method also includes an answer-format guard to prevent reward hacking through malformed or strategically formatted outputs.

A practical aspect of the method is that the PRM is used only during adaptation, not at deployment-time inference. The goal is to internalize process supervision into the policy parameters rather than rely on an external reranker or verifier at test time. That distinguishes PROSE from many inference-time selection methods, which often improve quality at the cost of additional runtime modules and compute.2b:T836,The paper first shows the failure mode of standard TTRL on medical multiple-choice QA: sample-level accuracy improves only slightly, majority-vote accuracy stays flat, and output diversity drops sharply. In other words, the model becomes more self-consistent but not more correct.

The most convincing experiment is the controlled answer-space intervention. On AMC math problems, the authors keep the questions, model, optimizer, and training procedure fixed, and vary only the answer format: original free-form answering versus a matched four-option multiple-choice version. In the free-form setting, TTRL improves steadily. In the multiple-choice setting, accuracy rises briefly and then collapses. This strongly supports the claim that answer-space structure, not domain difficulty, is the main cause of failure.

The paper also introduces the protection gap, which measures the difference between pseudo-label reward accuracy and true answer accuracy. In free-form answer spaces, this gap provides some protection because wrong answers disperse. In small answer spaces, the gap nearly disappears, making it much easier for wrong trajectories to be rewarded through majority agreement. This quantitative analysis supports the Lucky Hit diagnosis.

Empirically, PROSE improves both sample-level and majority-vote accuracy relative to standard TTRL and avoids the rapid diversity collapse. Two additional findings matter. First, the gains remain after removing the PRM at inference time, indicating that the policy has absorbed the process signal. Second, the gains transfer to datasets that were not used during adaptation, suggesting that the method learns more general reasoning behavior rather than dataset-specific answer preferences. The paper also compares reward aggregation schemes and reports that minimum aggregation is clearly better than mean aggregation, which is more vulnerable to reward hacking. The provided excerpt does not include full result tables, so it would be unsafe to fabricate exact per-dataset numbers, but the mechanism and empirical conclusions are already quite clear.2c:T5c7,This paper is worth reading carefully for its problem setup and experimental design, though the medical background itself does not need close attention. Start with the introduction and motivation sections, focusing on how the authors separate domain difficulty from answer-space structure. Then go directly to the AMC controlled experiment and the definition of the protection gap. Those are the most informative parts of the paper.

In the method section, pay attention to three things: how the PRM is trained or constructed, why trajectory reward uses the minimum step score rather than a mean or weighted sum, and what kinds of reward hacking are blocked by the answer-format guard. In the experiments, prioritize Figure 1, Figure 2, and the reward-aggregation ablations. If time is limited, about 40 minutes should be enough to capture the main ideas.

Three follow-up questions are worth deeper thought. First, how far does this result generalize beyond medical QA—to other MCQ settings, code selection tasks, or judge-based preference optimization with small label spaces? Second, minimum aggregation reduces reward hacking, but it may also over-penalize long reasoning chains with one noisy local step; the paper should be checked for reward-variance and stability analysis. Third, using the PRM only during adaptation is operationally attractive, but it also means PRM errors are written into the policy. That makes cross-distribution robustness an important next test.2d:T6f9,GrowMTP trains a multi-token draft head inside the RL loop using speculative verification signals. The main challenge is not the existence of labels, but how to make those labels valid. The paper identifies three constraints. First, supervision must be applied under rollout-consistent draft-conditioned states. The k-th drafted token is predicted conditioned on previous drafted tokens, not on the final executed prefix after verification. To avoid train-test mismatch, GrowMTP records the draft trajectory and reconstructs the hidden states corresponding to the actual draft-conditioned context that produced each proposal. Second, learning across drafting depths is coupled. A deeper token only matters if earlier drafted tokens are accepted, so depth-wise training should reflect that dependency rather than treating each position as an independent classification target. The paper introduces a depth-coupled acceptance (DCA) loss based on an acceptance-chain surrogate, with a logarithmic transform to increase relative gradient weight on low-chain-value cases. Third, supervision beyond the first rejection is invalid for execution, because deeper drafted tokens are conditioned on a rejected token and therefore lie off the executed prefix. GrowMTP gates the loss after the first rejection and excludes those suffix positions. A further design choice is to detach draft-head updates from the policy backbone, so the online drafting module does not directly perturb policy learning. This sacrifices possible gains from joint optimization, but keeps the method modular and stable. An important practical point is that no offline warm start or depth curriculum is required: the first draft position always yields valid supervision, so training can begin from random initialization.2e:T4a3,The headline result is from-scratch online growth on a headless model. On Qwen3-4B with a randomly initialized K=5 draft head, trained for 500 math RL steps, the acceptance length rises to 2.91 from the autoregressive floor. This yields 2.13x rollout speedup and 1.60x end-to-end step speedup, with head-training cost included. Over the same 500-step budget, GrowMTP reduces total GPU-hours by 9.4% relative to using an offline-trained head with comparable acceptance, which is the key economic result. The paper also studies online adaptation when a draft head already exists. On Qwen3.5-4B-Base, online updates provide an additional 1.36x rollout speedup over a frozen pretrained head. On MiMo-7B-SFT, extending the head from K=1 to K=5 increases acceptance from 1.65 to 4.0 and improves rollout speed by 1.93x. Another useful finding is domain specialization: heads trained on math rollouts and code rollouts achieve higher in-domain acceptance, supporting the claim that rollout-specific adaptation matters. The introduction does not expose all ablation numbers, but the experimental structure appears to cover from-scratch growth, continued online adaptation, and domain dependence.2f:T46d,Read this paper with a systems question in mind: why has speculative decoding been standard in serving, yet still depended on offline draft-head preparation in RL? After the introduction, focus on three technical pieces: reconstruction of draft-conditioned states, the DCA loss, and loss gating after the first rejection. Those are the parts that make the method more than simple distillation from verification logits. If time is limited, start with the method figure, the loss definition, and the rollout/training pseudocode, then move to the main training curves showing how acceptance length grows over RL steps and when net speedup appears. The most informative ablations are likely: removing draft-conditioned reconstruction, replacing DCA with independent depth losses, and training beyond the first rejection. While reading, it is worth asking whether the same online-growth idea could extend to stronger native MTP or EAGLE-style drafting modules, and whether partial backbone updates would help or destabilize RL. Around 20-30 minutes is enough for a solid skim; a careful read of method and experiments takes about an hour.30:T959,方法设计很干净。作者不从自然语言任务入手，而是构造一组 edge-emitting HMM，让模型读取由这些 HMM 生成的 token 序列，并预测下一个 token。对于每个时间步，真实 belief state 都可以由 HMM 的转移与发射概率精确算出，因此有了一个外部可监督的、理论上正确的表示目标。

论文的第一步是任务筛选。作者在 40 个具有非平凡 belief 结构的 HMM 上评估六个开源 LLM，只保留那些模型已经能接近最优 next-token 预测的过程。这样做是为了避免 probe 结果被“模型根本没学会这个过程”污染。换句话说，他们不是在问任何 HMM 上都能不能读出 belief state，而是在问：当模型行为上已经接近最优时，内部是否真的形成了对应的状态表示。

第二步是线性 probe。作者从不同层的 residual stream 提取激活，用线性回归去预测 belief state 向量，并报告 R2。这里的关键不是单纯 probe 成功，而是 belief state 的几何结构在多个模型、多个 HMM、多个层位上都能被线性读出，说明这种表示不是高度非线性、零散分布的偶然编码，而更像是残差空间中的一个相对规整子空间。

第三步是因果干预。论文做了两类 intervention：patching 和 steering。patching 可以理解为把某个样本在 belief 子空间上的表示替换成另一个表示，再看输出分布如何变化；steering 则是在该子空间上定向移动激活。作者比较了“替换成正确 belief 点”“替换成无关 belief 点”以及未干预模型的预测质量。若 belief 子空间只是相关但无功能，干预不应系统性改变输出；但实验显示，沿 probe 识别出的子空间做正确干预时，模型预测仍接近未干预水平，而替换成不相关 simplex 点会明显恶化。这说明该子空间对下游 next-token prediction 有因果作用。

理论背景上，作者借用了 computational mechanics。这个框架认为，对一个部分可观测随机过程做最优预测，需要维护能压缩过去并保留未来预测信息的充分统计量；在 HMM 场景下，这个量正是 belief state。论文的贡献不是提出新理论，而是把这个理论从 toy transformer 扩展到现成开源 LLM，并给出可证伪的表示预测、probe 和 intervention 证据链。31:Ta4a,The setup is clean and theory-driven. Instead of using natural-language tasks with ambiguous latent structure, the authors construct edge-emitting HMMs and feed their emitted token sequences to pretrained LLMs. For every time step, the ground-truth belief state can be computed exactly from the HMM dynamics, so the paper has a precise external target for what the model should represent if it is doing optimal latent-state tracking.

The first step is process selection. The authors evaluate six open-source LLMs on many HMMs and keep 40 with non-trivial belief structure on which the models achieve near-optimal next-token prediction. This is an important choice. They are not asking whether belief states are decodable for arbitrary HMMs; they are asking whether, when the model already behaves close to optimally, the corresponding latent posterior is present in the activations.

The second step is linear probing. Residual-stream activations from different layers are used to predict the belief-state vector with linear probes, and performance is measured with R2. The significance is not merely that a probe works. The belief geometry is linearly recoverable across multiple models, multiple HMMs, and at different depths, which suggests that the representation is organized in a relatively structured subspace rather than being diffusely encoded in a highly nonlinear way.

The third step is causal intervention. The paper uses patching and steering targeted at the probe-identified belief subspace. Roughly, patching replaces the belief-subspace component of one activation with another, while steering moves the activation along directions in that subspace. The authors compare interventions that preserve the correct belief geometry against interventions that substitute an unrelated point on the simplex. If the subspace were merely correlated with prediction, these manipulations should not systematically matter. Instead, interventions aligned with the identified belief state preserve prediction quality near the untampered model, while unrelated substitutions degrade it substantially. That makes the case functional rather than purely correlational.

The theoretical framing comes from computational mechanics. In that view, optimal prediction for a partially observed stochastic process requires tracking a sufficient statistic that compresses the past while preserving predictive information about the future. For HMMs, that statistic is exactly the belief state. The paper’s contribution is to carry this prediction from toy models into pretrained open-source LLMs and test it with both probes and interventions.32:T744,The study covers six open-source LLMs and 40 HMMs. The headline result is that belief states are linearly decodable from residual-stream activations, with peak probe R2 values ranging from 0.83 to 0.99 depending on the model and process. The best layer is not fixed: for some model–HMM pairs the strongest signal appears relatively early, for others later. That suggests belief-state formation is distributed and architecture-dependent rather than confined to the final layers.

A strong part of the experimental design is the behavioral filter. The authors first verify that the model is already near-optimal on next-token prediction for the selected HMMs, and only then analyze representations. This helps separate “the model never learned the process” from “the model learned it but represents it differently.”

The intervention results are the main functional evidence. When patching or steering within the identified belief subspace in a way that preserves the appropriate belief geometry, the KL divergence to the ground-truth next-token distribution remains on the same order as the untampered model. When the activation is replaced with an unrelated point on the belief simplex, prediction quality drops substantially. The abstract does not list all detailed numbers, but the qualitative pattern is consistent and important: correct manipulations of the belief subspace preserve behavior, incorrect ones damage it.

Another good design choice is the use of HMMs with non-trivial belief structure, rather than processes where the next-token distribution already fully captures the hidden dynamics. The paper also includes a contrasting case, Mess3, where hidden-state dynamics are no richer than the observable predictive distribution. That helps rule out the simpler interpretation that the probe is only reading out shallow token statistics.33:T4b8,Read this one with a specific question in mind: is the paper showing a real mechanism or just a probe artifact? Start with the introduction and Figure 1 to understand the exact hypothesis: not generic latent-state encoding, but belief-state geometry predicted by computational mechanics. Then read the background section on HMMs and belief states carefully so the probe target is unambiguous.

The most important part of the main text is the experimental design: how the 40 HMMs are selected, why near-optimal behavioral performance is required before probing, where in the residual stream the probes are applied, and what the control conditions are. Without those details, the probe results are easy to overinterpret.

After that, focus on the intervention section. The patching and steering constructions, and the use of KL divergence to the ground-truth next-token distribution as the evaluation metric, are what elevate the paper from representational correlation to something closer to mechanistic evidence. A careful first pass should take about 35–50 minutes. If you care about ICL theory or interpretability, the appendix on HMM selection, controls, and layerwise variation is worth the extra time.34:T43a,The experiments use Llama-3.1-8B-Instruct, Qwen3-8B, and Gemma-4-E4B. Activation analysis uses four depth checkpoints per model: layers 8/16/24/31 for Llama-3.1-8B, 9/18/27/35 for Qwen3-8B, and 28/29/40/41 for Gemma-4-E4B. Each condition uses 100 OpenOrca prompts of roughly 100 to 300 words, with adjacent-key typos applied at up to four positions. The observed on-site rotations are 43 to 56 degrees and decay to below 15% within about 10 downstream tokens. A stacked typo bundle reduces single-position prompt-injection probe TPR by 12.0 percentage points at FPR=1%, and threshold recalibration is not sufficient. Multi-position aggregation reduces localized perturbation loss to at most 0.5 percentage points, but distributed perturbations still cause about a 3.8-point drop even with attention- or max-based aggregation. The KV-cache-forked probe suffix closes about 95% of the single-position gap, leaving a 0.6-point residual, while perturbation-augmented training leaves a 3.7-point residual. An LLM-as-judge check labels 94.6% of clean-perturbed pairs as intent-preserving.15:["$","main",null,{"className":"flex-1 overflow-y-auto","children":["$","div",null,{"className":"mx-auto max-w-4xl px-4 py-8 sm:px-6 xl:max-w-6xl","children":["$","$L16",null,{"data":{"date":"2026-09-17","generated_at":"2026-09-17T01:45:35.370786+00:00","stats":{"total":106,"starred":4,"must_read":6,"skim":44,"glance":56,"by_category":{"pretraining_data":1,"industry":1,"llm_pretrain":5,"scaling":1,"architecture":5,"efficient":18,"eval":15,"multimodal":7,"multimodal_gen":4,"post_train":19,"mechanism":16,"coding_agent":4,"agent":10},"fetch_status":{}},"categories":[{"id":"pretraining_data","emoji":"📚","name":"预训练数据","description":"以预训练数据为核心贡献的工作：数据采集/清洗/去重、数据配比(data mixture)、数据筛选/质量评估、语料构建、数据课程(data curriculum)、数据重复(repetition)、合成数据用于预训练、Tokenizer/词表设计、成员推断/数据检测。判据是『论文核心是数据本身』，而非训练 recipe/优化器(→ llm_pretrain)或评测资产(→ eval)","papers":[{"id":"2609.16984","title":"Nameless Tokenization: A Lossless Tokenizer-Level Defense Against Control-Token Forgery in Open-Weight LLMs","abstract":"Open-weight language models publish the strings their chat templates use to mark turns, roles and tool results, which the tokenizer maps back to the reserved identifiers the model obeys. Standard practice treats it as a scalar penalty on the softmax log-normalizer, mainly used to keep logits bounded and reduce mixed-precision instability. The paper argues that this view misses the operative object: the gradient injected at the logit boundary. Once transported through the output projection, tied embeddings, top-k routing, fused loss implementations, and optimizer state, this gradient can have effects that are not predicted by the reported scalar Z-loss curve.","importance":"The main value for pretraining is a diagnostic framework for a stability regularizer that is already used in large-scale LM and MoE training. The contribution is not a universal coefficient or a single better Z-loss variant. It is a way to separate source amplitude, source shape, output-to-hidden gain, pathway coupling, and router effective scale. This matters because reducing log Z is not the same as reducing update tails or improving static loss-scale headroom. For large pretraining runs, the paper points to a practical rule: auxiliary stability losses should be audited at the level of backward sources and optimizer-facing updates, not only by their forward scalar values.","technical_approach":"$17","experiments":"The empirical study uses pretrained GPT-2-family models: GPT-2, DistilGPT-2, GPT-2 Medium, and GPT-2 Large, roughly 124M, 82M, 355M, and 774M parameters. The text data includes WikiText-103 and FineWeb-Edu. The experiments include matched dense continued pretraining, high-coefficient stress sweeps without relying on clipping to hide failures, static mixed-precision endpoint tests, low-rank output-to-hidden gain spectral audits, optimizer-facing update audits, audits of a row-wise fused CE+Z-loss Triton GPU kernel, and end-to-end top-k MoE language-model training. The main empirical message is that low-coefficient Z-loss settings can preserve validation perplexity, but stress regimes reveal model- and regime-dependent trade-offs among validation quality, update tails, Adam-state pressure, and mixed-precision headroom. In MoE training, router effective scale is a central variable; coefficients should be matched to routing and reduction details rather than copied from dense-head recipes.","reading_guide":"Read the Introduction and Backward Transport View carefully; they contain the conceptual shift from scalar penalty to transported backward source. Figure 1 is the map of the diagnostic framework. The common-shift and centering derivations are worth close attention because they explain why a CE-invisible logit direction can still be shaped by Z-loss. The tied-embedding gradient decomposition is important for modern LM implementations with shared embeddings. For MoE work, focus on top-k router scale distortion and scale matching. Extending reasoning across generation chunks normally requires keeping earlier generated text in context. We ask whether a dLLM can instead continue reasoning after that text is cleared, using only a fixed-size carried state. reas","starred":true,"priority":"p1","reading_tier":"must_read","source":"arxiv","sources":["arxiv"],"published_at":"2026-09-14T21:34:15Z","category_id":"architecture","cover_url":"/covers/2609_16372.png","hf_upvotes":0,"arxiv_categories":["cs.CL"],"summary":{"core_problem":"这项工作解决 diffusion language model 的一个关键短板：模型可以并行去噪生成一个 chunk，但跨 chunk 的长程推理通常仍要保留前面生成的文本。作者问的是，能否把已经生成的推理文本清掉，只携带固定大小的连续状态，让 dLLM 在后续 chunk 中继续推理。","importance":"这是值得精读的建模范式工作。它不是给 dLLM 加一个普通缓存，而是利用 dLLM 的双向注意力让固定位置 token 同时可读、可写，形成一种 bounded-state reasoning 机制。对预训练研究有三点启发。第一，AR 模型中的早期 token 或 memory token 多数是读多写少，dLLM 的 register token 可以在迭代去噪中反复更新，这给连续思维状态提供了更自然的接口。第二，长程推理不一定只能靠可见文本链，也可以靠训练出来的隐状态链；这会影响未来的 latent reasoning、any-order LM 和 diffusion LM 训练目标设计。第三，作者清楚指出了训练中的 shortcut 问题：如果 prompt 或当前 chunk 的部分 token 太容易被读取，模型不会学会使用 registers。因此训练目标必须主动制造对 register 的依赖。","technical_approach":"基础模型是 masked dLLM。训练时对 completion token 按时间 t 采样 mask，目标是在 masked positions 上预测原 token；推理时从全 mask 的长度 C chunk 开始，多步并行预测并保留高置信 token，直到 chunk 填满。register 机制是在 prompt 中放置 R 个固定位置的 register tokens。完成一个 chunk 后，模型额外跑一次 forward pass，读取这些 register 位置最后一层 hidden states，得到 R×d 的连续状态。生成下一个 chunk 时，把这些保存的 hidden states 重新注入到相同 register 位置的输入 embedding 中，其他 prompt token 保持不变。位置复用意味着模型必须学会在同一组 slots 中覆盖旧信息、保留关键状态，并写入下一步推理所需的信息。训练 recipe 是关键。给定 prompt 和长 reasoning trace，作者把 trace 切成多个 chunks，训练模型在生成当前 chunk 后清除可见文本，只保留 register 状态，再预测下一 chunk。为了避免模型绕过 register，训练中会随机阻断新 chunk 对 prompt 的注意力，并强制一些 pass 从 fully masked state 预测 chunk。作者还区分两类目标：task-directed registers 用 next-chunk loss 直接训练对任务有用的状态；memory tokens 则用重构前一 chunk 的目标训练，作为偏记忆压缩的对照。最后，作者把 registers 接入 chunked GRPO，用 RL 继续改善跨 chunk 携带状态。","experiments":"实验在 LLaDA 和 Dream 两类 dLLM 上进行，比较对象包括 full-sequence SFT without carry、离散文本 carry、以及 reconstruction-trained memory tokens。主结果是，registers 在所有主要比较中都超过 discrete-text carry，并且在 12 个 carry-baseline 对比中领先 10 个。优势在代码任务上最明显，原因是成功程序往往跨越多个 chunks，需要携带中间控制流、变量状态和局部计划；固定长度的离散文本 carry 容易丢掉这些结构。作者还在 Countdown 和 LongArithmetic 上加入 chunked GRPO，进一步提升多 chunk 任务表现，说明 register 状态不仅能通过 SFT 学到，也能被 RL 信号继续塑形。原文可见部分没有给出完整参数量、chunk size、register 数量和逐 benchmark 分数；已经给出的可核验数字是 R×d 的状态形式、chunk 长度记为 C、主比较 10/12 领先、以及 registers 对 discrete-text carry 的全覆盖优势。","reading_guide":"建议精读 Methods 和实验消融，预计 60 到 90 分钟。先看 Figure 1，确认 bounded-state multi-chunk reasoning 的设定：生成文本被清除，状态大小不随生成长度增长。再看 Figure 2 和方法部分的 inference pseudocode，重点理解 register hidden states 如何保存、注入、复用。最值得细读的是训练 recipe：清除前文、保留 registers、prompt attention masking、fully masked prediction，这些设计决定模型是否真的使用连续状态。实验部分优先看三类对照：discrete text carry、reconstruction memory tokens、full-sequence SFT。最后看 GRPO 部分，关注 RL 是提升 register 的读写策略，还是只是提升局部 chunk 生成质量。若要继续做研究，最值得深挖的问题是 register 容量随 R 和 d 的 scaling、状态可解释性、跨任务泛化，以及能否把这种目标前移到预训练或 mid-training 阶段。","authors_note":""},"summary_en":{"core_problem":"The work addresses a core limitation of diffusion language models: they can denoise a chunk in parallel, but long reasoning across chunks usually The central question is whether a dLLM can clear that text and continue reasoning from a fixed-size continuous state.","importance":"This is a strong modeling-paradigm contribution for diffusion LMs and latent reasoning. The key idea is not a conventional cache. It uses bidirectional attention in dLLMs to make fixed-position tokens both readable and writable during denoising. That differs from causal LMs, where early memory-like positions are mostly read-only during later decoding. The result suggests a practical interface for bounded-state reasoning: reasoning progress can be carried by learned continuous registers rather than by visible chain-of-thought text. For pretraining research, the most useful lesson is the training pressure: registers only become useful when the objective prevents shortcuts through the prompt or the current partially unmasked chunk.","technical_approach":"$18","experiments":"The experiments are run on LLaDA and Dream. The clearest gains appear on code tasks, where successful generations often span several chunks and require carrying variable state, control-flow decisions, and partial plans. A fixed window of discrete text is a weak carrier for that information. Register-based chunked GRPO further improves performance on Countdown and LongArithmetic, indicating that the continuous state can be shaped by RL signals after supervised training. 的上下文是双向耦合的。若论文对错误传播分析不足，这会是后续复现时需要重点验证的风险点。","authors_note":""},"summary_en":{"core_problem":"The paper targets a practical bottleneck in diffusion LLM inference. Although dLLMs generate tokens in parallel through iterative denoising, they still require many denoising steps before token confidence crosses the decoding threshold. As a result, many tokens that are already effectively correct at early steps keep being recomputed. Existing acceleration methods usually rely on fixed block sizes or fixed confidence thresholds, which implicitly assume roughly uniform token difficulty across positions. The paper argues that this assumption is wrong: token difficulty is highly heterogeneous, and tokens with similar uncertainty often cluster together. The core problem is therefore how to identify groups of tokens that can be finalized earlier, before the standard confidence threshold, without noticeably hurting generation quality.","importance":"This is not a pretraining-method paper in the narrow sense. It is an inference-side efficiency paper for diffusion LMs. Still, it matters for foundation-model research because diffusion LM adoption depends heavily on whether its theoretical parallelism can be converted into real throughput. If inference remains too step-heavy, the modeling paradigm stays academically interesting but operationally weak. The paper’s main contribution is to move beyond hand-designed heuristics and treat token difficulty as something to be learned during decoding. That is a useful direction for dLLM inference research, where many methods still focus on cache engineering or sampler tweaks rather than learning which tokens should converge together and which ones can be safely frozen early. The contribution is meaningful but should be read as a solid decoding optimization paper, not as a change to the core language-modeling paradigm.","technical_approach":"$1b","experiments":"$1c","reading_guide":"$1d","authors_note":""},"recommendation":{"zh":"如果你在跟 diffusion LM，这篇该看；不是范式突破，但它直指“并行生成却不快”这个老问题，先看方法图和 latency table，质量是否稳住比加速倍数更关键。","en":"Worth a look if you track diffusion LMs: not a paradigm shift, but it attacks the old 'parallel yet still slow' problem; read the method figure and latency tables first, and check quality retention before trusting the speedup."},"metadata":{"institutions":[],"authors":["Lixuan Wei","Wei Zhou","Jianwen Wu","Yipeng Shen","Meiling Wang","Haoran You"],"notable_authors":[],"tags":["diffusion-lm","parallel-decoding","inference-acceleration","adaptive-blocking","sampling"],"links":{"arxiv":"https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.16450","pdf":"https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.16450","github":null,"project":null,"source_url":null,"article":null}}},{"id":"2609.16229","title":"Test-Time Unlearning via Sparse Autoencoder","abstract":"Machine unlearning aims to remove specific knowledge from a trained large language model (LLM) without retraining from scratch. Existing methods modify model weights via gradient ascent and its advances. While effective on certain benchmarks, these weight-based approaches exhibit a sharp forget-utility trade-off, where stronger forgetting of target knowledge can degrade model utility, and unlearned knowledge may reappear under post-unlearning fine-tuning or prompt attacks. We propose ARIA (autoe","starred":false,"priority":"p2","reading_tier":"skim","source":"arxiv","sources":["arxiv"],"published_at":"2026-09-14T18:58:09Z","category_id":"efficient","cover_url":"/covers/2609_16229.png","hf_upvotes":0,"arxiv_categories":["cs.LG","cs.CL"],"summary":{"core_problem":"这篇工作处理的是 LLM 机器遗忘里一个很难绕开的矛盾：如果用改权重的方式去“删知识”，往往会把目标知识附近的正常能力一起伤到；但如果改得轻，知识又会在攻击提示、软提示、后续微调，甚至量化后重新冒出来。论文把问题重述为“运行时访问控制”而不是“参数层面的永久擦除”：不去要求一次全局参数更新同时兼顾遗忘和保留，而是在生成过程中识别模型何时进入与待遗忘知识相关的内部状态，只在这些状态上做局部干预。这样，遗忘与保留不再被绑在同一组权重更新上。","importance":"对预训练和基座模型研究的意义不在于它提出了新的训练 recipe，而在于它给“知识位于哪里、如何被访问、如何被选择性阻断”提供了一个更可操作的视角。第一，它说明很多所谓 unlearning 的困难，未必都来自知识已经深埋在参数里，也可能来自我们缺少对“何时访问该知识”的状态级控制。第二，它把 SAE 特征、可解释中间表征、推理时控制三件事接到了一起，这对后续做安全控制、能力路由、知识隔离、甚至 post-train 与 pretrain 接口研究都有启发。第三，文中给出的结论很务实：如果检测器误差可控，forget leakage 和 retain degradation 都能被界定到检测误差上；这比很多只看最终 benchmark 分数的 unlearning 工作更接近工程上可部署的分析框架。量级上，它更像是一个方法范式上的推进，而不是直接改写预训练主线的工作。","technical_approach":"方法核心是 ARIA：Autoencoder-gated Inference-time Unlearning。整体分三步。第一步，作者先在通用语料上预训练 sparse autoencoder，把基座模型若干层的激活分解成稀疏 latent feature。这里选 SAE 而不是直接用原始 hidden state，原因是 SAE latent 更稀疏、更可解释，也更适合后面训练一个线性检测头；如果直接在 dense hidden state 上做检测，虽然也可能有效，但触发依据会更难审计，且更容易把很多无关方向混在一起。第二步，用 forget/retain 相关轨迹在 SAE latent 空间里训练一个轻量的稀疏线性 detector，判断当前生成状态是否进入“与待遗忘知识相关”的区域。这个 detector 不是对输入 prompt 做分类，而是对生成过程中的状态做逐步判别，因此它能捕捉中间推理、改写、旁敲侧击等更隐蔽的泄露路径。第三步，在推理时做 detector-gated policy intervention：当 detector 未触发时，完全沿用原模型分布；当 detector 触发时，对 next-token policy 做抑制式干预，阻断目标知识暴露。论文强调这种设计与权重编辑的区别：权重编辑是全局、静态、一次性的；ARIA 是局部、条件化、按状态触发的。理论部分给出一个关键结论，即遗忘泄露和保留损伤都受 detector 的误报/漏报率控制。直观上，漏报会让该拦截的状态漏过去，形成 forget leakage；误报会把本来正常的状态也拦住，形成 retain degradation。这个分解很重要，因为它把问题从“如何找到完美的权重更新”转成“如何学到更准的状态检测器”。论文还利用 detector 的稀疏性做 feature-level case study，发现部分 retain 下降并不是知识本身被误伤，而是 unlearning 数据里的回答风格偏差被 detector 学进去了，这一点对 benchmark 构造很有提醒意义。","experiments":"实验覆盖三个常见遗忘基准：TOFU、R-TOFU、WMDP，并在两类模型上验证：一个是 thinking model DeepSeek-R1-1.5B，一个是 instruction model Gemma-3-1B-it。作者把 ARIA 与主流 weight-based unlearning 方法做对比，结论是 forget-retain trade-off 更好，而且在更强攻击下更稳。摘要和引言里给出的代表性结果是：在 WMDP-cyber 上，ARIA 能把 forget-set accuracy 压到所有对比方法之下，同时把 MMLU 控制在相对原模型约 1% 的下降内。这个结果说明它不是靠大面积降能力来“假装遗忘”。更关键的是鲁棒性评估。作者专门设计了三类 post-unlearning adversarial attacks，覆盖权重空间和解码空间：包括让模型在遗忘后再做轻量微调、通过提示或解码策略恢复被压制行为等。ARIA 因为不改原始权重，攻击面与权重编辑法不同，实验里显示它在这些恢复攻击下仍能维持较好的遗忘效果。消融层面，值得注意的不是单一数字，而是几个趋势：一是 SAE latent 上的稀疏线性 detector 已经足够形成有效边界，说明 forget-related state 在表征空间里确实有可分性；二是 retain 下降与 detector 误报更相关，而不是与干预强度线性对应；三是 feature case study 暗示 benchmark 中“风格模式”和“知识内容”可能纠缠，导致一些方法看起来在遗忘，实际是在学会拒答或改变措辞。若从实验设计质量看，这篇工作的亮点是没有只停留在标准 forget/retain 分数，而是把恢复攻击和可解释分析一起做了。","reading_guide":"泛读建议是先抓三处。第一，看引言和方法开头，理解作者为什么把 unlearning 改写成 runtime routing 问题，而不是继续在 weight edit 上打补丁。第二，看 detector-gated inference policy 和理论界限那一节，重点理解误报/漏报如何分别对应 retain degradation 与 forget leakage；这是整篇最有方法论价值的部分。第三，看实验里的攻击设置和 feature-level case study，这两部分决定了它是否只是 benchmark 技巧。若时间有限，优先看 Figure 2 一类的方法总览图、理论命题、WMDP 结果表，以及分析 retain degradation 来源的可解释案例。预计 30–45 分钟可以建立完整判断；如果要深挖，可继续看 SAE 训练细节、检测器稀疏性约束、以及不同层 latent 的触发分布，判断这种方法能否迁移到更大模型或更复杂的知识域。","authors_note":""},"summary_en":{"core_problem":"The paper tackles a persistent failure mode in LLM unlearning: weight-editing methods must use one global parameter update to both suppress target knowledge and preserve nearby capabilities, which creates a sharp forget–utility trade-off. If the edit is strong, general utility drops; if it is mild, the supposedly removed knowledge can often be recovered through adversarial prompts, soft prompts, post-unlearning fine-tuning, or even quantization. The paper reframes unlearning as a runtime access-control problem over a frozen model: instead of trying to erase knowledge from weights, detect when generation enters a forget-related internal state and intervene only there.","importance":"Its value for foundation-model research is less about a new pretraining recipe and more about a cleaner decomposition of the problem. It suggests that part of the unlearning difficulty is not only where knowledge is stored, but when and how that knowledge is accessed during generation. It also connects three threads that are increasingly relevant to base-model work: sparse interpretable features, activation-level control, and selective policy intervention at inference time. That makes it relevant beyond unlearning, including safety control, capability routing, knowledge isolation, and the broader pretrain–posttrain interface. The contribution is best read as a methodological shift in how to operationalize selective forgetting, not as a direct change to mainstream pretraining.","technical_approach":"$1e","experiments":"$1f","reading_guide":"For a skim-level read, focus on three parts. First, read the motivation and method overview to understand why the paper treats unlearning as runtime routing instead of another variant of weight editing. Second, read the detector-gated policy section together with the theorem that links false positives and false negatives to retain degradation and forget leakage; this is the conceptual center of the paper. Third, read the adversarial recovery evaluation and the feature-level case study, since these determine whether the method is more than a benchmark-specific trick. If time is limited, prioritize the main method figure, the theorem, the WMDP results table, and the interpretability analysis of residual retain loss. Around 30–45 minutes should be enough to form a solid view. A deeper read should inspect SAE training details, sparsity constraints on the detector, and which layers contribute most to trigger decisions.","authors_note":""},"recommendation":{"zh":"利用 SAE 在推理时进行干预以实现机器遗忘，避免了修改权重带来的副作用，为控制模型输出提供了一种轻量级且可解释的途径。","en":"Utilizes SAEs for inference-time intervention to achieve machine unlearning, avoiding the side effects of weight modification, providing a lightweight and interpretable way to control model outputs."},"metadata":{"institutions":[],"authors":["Pingzhi Li","Jinhao Duan","Vaishnav Tadiparthi","Nakul Agarwal","Kwonjoon Lee","Ehsan Moradi Pari","Hossein Nourkhiz Mahjoub","Sijia Liu","Tianlong Chen"],"notable_authors":["Sijia Liu","Tianlong Chen"],"tags":["machine unlearning","test-time intervention","sparse autoencoder","forget-utility trade-off"],"links":{"arxiv":"https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.16229","pdf":"https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.16229","github":null,"project":null,"source_url":null,"article":null}}},{"id":"2609.17184","title":"LoopSpec: Pipelined Self-Speculative Decoding for Looped Transformers","abstract":"Looped Transformers achieve strong performance with compact parameter sizes by repeatedly applying a shared stack of Transformer blocks across recurrent depths. However, they incur higher decoding latency than standard Transformer models of comparable parameter size because shared weights are accessed at every recurrent depth. To improve decoding efficiency, self-speculative decoding is particularly well suited to Looped Transformers, as their intermediate recurrent states can directly provide d","starred":false,"priority":"p2","reading_tier":"skim","source":"arxiv","sources":["arxiv"],"published_at":"2026-09-15T13:43:31Z","category_id":"efficient","cover_url":"/covers/2609_17184.png","hf_upvotes":0,"arxiv_categories":["cs.LG","cs.CL"],"summary":{"core_problem":"这篇工作处理的是 Looped Transformer 的一个很具体但很关键的瓶颈：参数共享带来了更小的模型存储，但在自回归解码时，同一组循环层要在每个 token 上反复读取，推理容易受显存带宽限制，导致延迟偏高。已有 speculative decoding 往往依赖单独训练的 draft model，会引入额外训练成本、分布失配和显存占用。论文要解决的是：能否只利用 Looped Transformer 自身在中间 recurrent depth 产生的“半成品预测”，做严格无损、训练免费、且适配循环结构的 speculative decoding，并真正把流水线并行跑起来。","importance":"对基座研究的意义主要不在训练范式，而在架构—推理接口。Looped Transformer 这类共享层架构如果没有合适的解码机制，参数效率很难转化成真实服务效率。LoopSpec 给出的价值是：它把循环深度中的中间分布当成天然 drafter，用目标模型自身完成 draft 与 verify，避免了额外小模型；同时它不是简单套用已有 self-speculative decoding，而是针对 recurrent depth 的逐步精化特性设计了双提案和流水线调度。对做新架构的人，这篇论文回答了一个现实问题：共享参数模型怎样在不改训练、不加蒸馏的前提下，把推理吞吐做出来。","technical_approach":"$20","experiments":"实验覆盖两个 Looped Transformer 家族、七个 checkpoint，并在真实系统实现上报告端到端 lossless speedup。论文给出的最好结果是最高 6.83× 的无损加速；相对训练式方法 DFlash，最高可再快 1.7×。这说明在 Looped Transformer 这个场景里，利用中间深度直接做 self-speculation，比额外训练 drafter 更合适。\n\n从实验结构看，重点不是单一平均数，而是几组机制验证。第一，作者比较不同 proposal depth 组合，发现存在稳定最优区间，且与 TV distance 幂律衰减推导出的闭式最优解一致。第二，双提案相对单提案在第一提案被拒时显著减少完全回退带来的损失，说明 residual second proposal 不是锦上添花，而是流水线稳定收益的来源。第三，gating 在 acceptance 已高的设置下减少了额外 speculative 分支的浪费，带来更好的 speedup/compute 折中。第四，系统层面的 branch-wise KV 管理和 CUDA Graph 对最终速度贡献明显，否则算法层面的理论收益很难落到端到端延迟上。\n\n从论文给出的结论可以读出一个更细的经验规律：LoopSpec 的收益依赖中间深度分布与最终分布的接近程度。如果较浅 recurrent state 已经有不错的 token agreement，就能较早启动后续 token 计算；如果更深状态对最终分布收敛更快，第二提案就能有效兜底。因此不同 loop 架构、不同 recurrent depth 配置的收益会有差异，不能把 6.83× 直接外推到所有共享层模型。","reading_guide":"这篇适合泛读，但建议把方法和理论部分看扎实。优先看 Introduction 和 Figure 1，先建立对单提案、双提案、流水线执行顺序的直观理解。然后看 Section 4.2 和 4.3，那里是 residual second proposal 与 gating 的核心。若只看摘要，很容易把它误解成“多开一条 speculative 分支”，但正文里真正重要的是如何保证无偏采样、何时触发 fallback、以及为什么第二提案能保留进度。\n\n接着看 Section 5。这里的价值不只是一个公式，而是作者把中间深度到最终分布的 TV distance 衰减规律和最优深度选择联系起来。对做 Looped / recurrent / shared-weight 架构的人，这部分提供了一个可迁移的分析框架：中间层分布如果能稳定逼近最终层，就有机会做训练免费的 self-speculation。\n\n实验部分建议重点看 speedup 主表、proposal depth 消融、gating 消融，以及系统实现附录中 KV 管理细节。预计 30–45 分钟可以抓住主要贡献；如果你关心 serving 落地，附录值得再花时间。","authors_note":""},"summary_en":{"core_problem":"The paper targets a practical bottleneck of Looped Transformers. Weight sharing makes these models parameter-efficient, but autoregressive decoding repeatedly reloads the same recurrent block stack for every token, so latency becomes memory-bandwidth bound. Standard speculative decoding can help, but usually requires a separately trained draft model, which adds training cost, memory overhead, and draft-target mismatch. The question here is whether a Looped Transformer can use its own intermediate recurrent states as drafts, with exact sampling and no extra training, while turning the recurrent computation into a useful decoding pipeline rather than a sequential loop.","importance":"Its value is mainly at the architecture–inference boundary. Looped Transformers only become compelling in practice if their parameter efficiency can be converted into real decoding speed. LoopSpec shows that the recurrent-depth structure itself provides a natural self-speculation signal: intermediate distributions are progressively refined versions of the final distribution. sa","starred":false,"priority":"p2","reading_tier":"skim","source":"arxiv","sources":["arxiv"],"published_at":"2026-09-14T20:12:27Z","category_id":"eval","cover_url":"/covers/2609_16305.png","hf_upvotes":0,"arxiv_categories":["cs.AI","cs.CE","cs.CL","cs.LG","cs.MA"],"summary":{"core_problem":"这篇工作解决的不是 agent 能不能完成任务，而是长时程、可调用工具、带状态环境中的“安全校准”该怎么评。现有 agent safety 评测常把结果压成 attack success 或 task success 两个标量，但在真实系统里，风险往往是多轮累积出来的：用户意图会漂移，授权会变化，工具返回内容可能带攻击，环境状态也会改变。此时真正需要判断的是 agent 何时该继续执行、何时该拒绝、拒绝是否过度，以及这些决策是否和轨迹上下文一致。Blindspot 把评测单位从单轮回复改成完整 trajectory，试图测的是 agent 在长时程交互中的安全边界是否稳定、是否校准。","importance":"它对预训练本身不是直接贡献，但对 post-train、agent policy 学习、tool-use 数据构造和安全对齐评测很有现实意义。原因有两点。第一，很多模型在单轮安全 benchmark 上分数不差，但一旦进入多轮工具环境，失败模式会从“直接答错”变成“在若干看似合理的中间步骤后做出错误执行”。这种 failure 只有 trajectory-level benchmark 才能看见。第二，这篇工作强调 refusal calibration，而不是单纯压低 unsafe completion。对基座模型研究者来说，这提醒我们：如果后训练只优化拒答率或攻击成功率，模型很容易走向过度保守，损失 benign utility。Blindspot 提供的是更细粒度的评测框架，适合用来检验 agent-style post-training 是否真的学到了状态依赖的安全决策，而不是学会了泛化很差的模板化拒绝。","technical_approach":"Blindspot 的设计重点是“活的仿真框架”而不是静态题库。每个 scenario 都定义初始世界状态、可用工具、适用策略、用户目标和安全相关条件。评测时有一个 User Agent 与 Target Agent 多轮交互；对于 adaptive attack，User Agent 的下一步会根据 Target Agent 的真实响应和当前攻击状态动态生成，而不是预先写死脚本。Target Agent 在一个有状态的工具环境中执行操作，工具调用、返回结果、环境状态变化都会被记录下来。这样，评测就不再只看文本表面，而是能检查 agent 是否真的执行了不该执行的动作。论文当前版本包含 22 个 attack family、35 个 scenario、7 个 domain，其中 25 个是 core-domain，10 个是 cross-domain，后者要求 agent 处理跨系统、跨授权边界的依赖关系。数据规模上有 2500+ 条长轨迹，平均长度 14.7 turn。更关键的是判定协议。作者没有把结果只分成成功/失败，而是定义了五类 trajectory-level outcome：Safe Completion、Correct Refusal、Unsafe Completion、Over-Refusal、Indeterminate。前四类把“该做时做”“该拒时拒”“不该做却做了”“该做却拒了”区分开，Indeterminate 则留给工具故障、环境异常、执行不完整等无法下结论的情况。判定流程结合 policy/authorization/tool execution/environment state checks，再辅以 semantic judge 和必要的人审，目的是把“嘴上说不会做”和“实际上已经做了”区分开。这一点比很多只看最终文本的安全评测更扎实。","experiments":"实验评了 13 个 proprietary 和 open-weight 模型，用 8 个 trajectory-level metric 去刻画 unsafe completion、appropriate refusal、benign utility、over-refusal、execution incompleteness、repeated-run robustness、post-refusal failure 等维度。全文片段里没有给出完整数字表，但从作者总结可以看出几个明确结论。第一，模型安全不能用单一标量描述；有些模型 unsafe completion 低，但 over-refusal 高，实用性差；有些模型任务完成率高，但在授权变化、外部内容注入、连续施压等场景下会逐步越界。第二，长时程工具环境会暴露单轮 benchmark 看不到的失败模式，尤其是 intent hijacking、objective drift、memory poisoning、indirect prompt injection 一类需要多步累积的攻击。第三，重复运行稳定性和 refusal 后失败恢复也是重要维度，同一个模型在不同 rollout 中可能出现边界不一致，这对 agent 部署很关键。就实验价值而言，重点不在某个模型赢了多少，而在于 benchmark 把“安全”和“可用”拆成了可同时观察的多维结果。","reading_guide":"泛读时建议先看 benchmark 设计而不是模型榜单。优先读引言里对长时程安全问题的界定、scenario 与 attack family 的构成、以及五类 outcome 的定义。然后看 adjudication pipeline，确认它如何利用执行记录和环境状态避免只凭文本判断。最后再看 8 个 metric 和 13 个模型的结果分布，理解为什么单一 attack success rate 不够。若时间有限，抓住三张内容：任务框架图、outcome taxonomy、以及按模型展示 unsafe completion 与 over-refusal 张力的主结果图。预计 25–35 分钟可以完成有效泛读。若要进一步判断它是否适合作为训练反馈信号，可继续看 adaptive attacker 的生成方式、human review 介入比例、以及 cross-domain scenario 的构造细节。","authors_note":""},"summary_en":{"core_problem":"The paper addresses a gap in agent safety evaluation: in long-horizon, tool-using settings, safety is not just about whether an attack eventually succeeds or a task gets completed. Risk can emerge gradually through intent drift, changing authorization, adversarial tool outputs, or accumulated state transitions. What needs to be evaluated is whether the agent remains properly calibrated over the full trajectory: when it should continue acting, when it should refuse, whether it over-refuses, and whether those decisions stay consistent as context and environment evolve. Blindspot therefore treats the full interaction trajectory, not an isolated response, as the unit of evaluation.","importance":"This is not a pretraining contribution in the narrow sense, but it matters for post-training, tool-use policy learning, safety alignment, and evaluation design. Many models look acceptable on single-turn safety benchmarks yet fail in multi-step environments where the harmful outcome appears only after several individually plausible actions. Blindspot exposes those failures. Its second important contribution is to center refusal calibration rather than only minimizing unsafe completion. That is useful for foundation-model researchers because it highlights a common post-training failure mode: optimizing for refusal or attack resistance alone can produce brittle over-refusal and poor benign utility. The benchmark is therefore a better probe of whether an agent has learned state-dependent safety behavior rather than generic refusal templates.","technical_approach":"$24","experiments":"$25","reading_guide":"For a skim, start with the benchmark design rather than the model ranking. Read the problem setup for long-horizon safety, then the scenario and attack-family construction, and then the five-outcome taxonomy. After that, inspect the adjudication pipeline carefully, since it is what makes the benchmark execution-grounded rather than text-only. Finally, look at the metric definitions and the model result patterns to see why attack success alone is insufficient. Such rubrics are reliable only if they reward honest answers over adversarial answers optimized to exploit them. Yet their robustness to such optimization remains poorly understood. We isolate the hardest regime: impossible tasks, where the prompt pressures the model toward an unsupported conclusion, so the only honest response is to ","starred":false,"priority":"p2","reading_tier":"skim","source":"arxiv","sources":["arxiv"],"published_at":"2026-09-15T08:21:40Z","category_id":"eval","cover_url":"/covers/2609_16816.png","hf_upvotes":0,"arxiv_categories":["cs.LG","cs.CL"],"summary":{"core_problem":"论文要解决的问题很直接：当 rubric 由 LLM 自动生成，并被拿来做 reward、judge 或自动评分时，这个 rubric 会不会奖励“迎合 rubric 的错误答案”，而不是奖励真正诚实、证据一致的答案。作者把问题放在 impossible tasks 上来测，也就是题目会强迫模型给出一个结论，但给定的封闭证据包其实不支持这个结论；在这种场景里，正确行为不是编一个看似完整的答案，而是明确指出证据不足或任务要求与证据冲突。论文关心的不是 answer model 会不会 abstain，而是 rubric generator 生成出来的评分标准是否可被攻击、是否会把 reward signal 引向错误方向。","importance":"对预训练本身，它不是直接改数据或优化器，但对 pretrain 到 post-train 的接口很重要，尤其是 RL with rubric rewards、LLM-as-a-judge、自动化对齐流水线。启发点在于：一旦 reward 由生成式 rubric 提供，问题就不再只是 judge 噪声，而是 reward specification 本身可能系统性错位。对于做 RLVR、RLAIF、合成监督和自动评测的人，这篇工作提醒了一件很关键的事：如果 rubric 生成器在“证据不足但任务强迫给结论”的场景下会把 unsupported inference 写进评分标准，那么后续再强的 policy optimization 也只是在更高效地学坏。贡献类型上，这是一篇评测与失效分析工作，价值主要在于把 reward hacking 从 answer model 层面往前推进到 rubric generation 层面，并给出可复现实验环境和 oracle certificate。","technical_approach":"方法上，作者构建了 ImpossibleRubrics benchmark。它不是传统那种固定 rubric 数据集，而是保存 task environment：每个样本包含一个 closed evidence packet、一个会施压模型给出确定结论的问题、以及一个 machine-checkable oracle certificate，明确哪些 claim 被允许、哪些 claim 被禁止。这样做的原因很清楚：如果 benchmark 直接给固定 rubric，模型可能记住 rubric 模板，测不出生成器质量；而用 environment + certificate，就可以每次让不同 generator 现场生成 rubric，再测试这个 rubric 是否可被利用。评测协议也设计得比较严：先由 rubric generator 根据 task+evidence 生成 rubric；再让一个固定 attacker 去写一份尽量拿高分、但不一定证据忠实的答案；同时准备 honest baseline；然后用 literal judge 按 rubric 打分，并由 oracle certificate 判断答案是否违规。核心 exploitation 定义是：攻击答案在 rubric judge 下得分高于 honest baseline，但被 oracle 判为不合规。这样就把“rubric 可被攻击”从模糊印象变成了可计算事件。论文还做了多组 robustness analysis，试图区分问题到底出在 judge、oracle、attacker，还是确实出在 rubric generation 本身。引言里提到九项稳健性分析，以及用 fixed rubrics 做校准：certificate-faithful 的固定 rubric 在 45 个测试里 0 次被 exploit，这一点很关键，因为它说明失败不是任务天然太难，而是生成出来的 rubric 质量不够。","experiments":"基准规模是 169 个 impossible tasks，加上 48 个 answerable controls，覆盖六类场景。作者在 11 个 rubric generators 上做了 cross-vendor benchmark，观察到一个比较清楚的 exploit gradient：较强一档模型在 stress cut 上大约 8%-15% 被 exploit，中档约 17%-18%，mini 级别约 26%。这个结果有两个信息。第一，问题没有被更强模型完全解决，只是程度不同，说明 rubric generation 还远未饱和。第二，开源权重模型在这个 stress cut 上能接近同期闭源模型，意味着这里的瓶颈未必只是参数规模，也可能是任务 framing 和 reward specification 能力。论文给出的典型失败案例很有代表性：证据包里没有 prophylaxis trial，诚实答案应说明无法给出数值概率；但生成的 rubric 却把“0.0%”写成满分答案的一部分，于是攻击者顺着 rubric 编出一个看似 packet-faithful 的确定性数字，最终拿到 100/100，而 honest baseline 只有 36/100。oracle certificate 则能识别这种 unsupported inference。这个例子很好地说明了 failure mode：rubric 把“没有证据”误写成“证据支持零效应”。","reading_guide":"这篇适合先看 benchmark design 和 evaluation protocol，再看案例和 robustness。优先读 benchmark 定义：什么叫 impossible environment，certificate 如何编码 permitted/prohibited claims，为什么要保存 environment 而不是固定 rubric。然后看 exploitation protocol，弄清 attacker、literal judge、oracle 三者各自负责什么。实验部分重点看不同 generator 的 exploit rate 排名，以及 controls 上的表现，判断它是不是单纯更保守或更爱拒答。案例分析值得细看，尤其是作者展示的 ivermectin 例子，它把 rubric generation 的错误推理链暴露得很清楚。最后看 robustness analyses 和 fixed-rubric calibration，这部分决定结论是否站得住。泛读大约 25 分钟；如果你在做 RL reward design、judge system 或自动化 alignment pipeline，建议把附录里的 certificate 设计和 error taxonomy 也看掉，约 45-60 分钟。","authors_note":""},"summary_en":{"core_problem":"The paper asks a precise question: when rubrics are generated by language models and then used as rewards, judges, or grading criteria, do they reward honest evidence-faithful answers, or can they be exploited by answers optimized to satisfy the rubric while violating the underlying task objective? The authors study this on impossible tasks, where the prompt pressures the model toward a definite conclusion but the closed evidence packet does not support such a conclusion. In these settings, the correct behavior is often to acknowledge the evidence gap or the conflict between the request and the evidence. The target of evaluation is not the answer model alone, but the rubric generator itself.","importance":"This is not a pretraining-method paper, but it is highly relevant to the pretrain-to-posttrain interface, especially for RL with rubric-based rewards, LLM-as-a-judge pipelines, and automated alignment systems. The key lesson is that once the reward is mediated by a generated rubric, the failure mode is no longer just noisy judging. The reward specification itself can become misaligned in a systematic way. For anyone working on RLVR, RLAIF, synthetic supervision, or automated evaluation, the paper highlights a serious risk: if the rubric generator encodes unsupported inferences into the scoring criteria, stronger policy optimization will simply learn to exploit the wrong objective more efficiently. The contribution is mainly an evaluation and failure-analysis asset, but it sharpens the discussion around reward hacking by moving one level upstream, from answer generation to rubric generation.","technical_approach":"$26","experiments":"$27","reading_guide":"Start with the benchmark construction and the evaluation protocol. The most important design choice is the use of environments plus certificates rather than static rubrics. Then read the exploitation protocol carefully to understand the roles of the attacker, the literal judge, and the oracle. In the experiments, focus on exploit rates across generators and on the answerable controls, which help distinguish exploitability from simple over-refusal or conservatism. The case studies are worth close attention, especially the ivermectin example, because they make the rubric-generation failure concrete and easy to reason about. Finally, read the robustness analyses and the fixed-rubric calibration, since those sections determine whether the conclusions are well isolated.
